last: **GZIP WRITING LANDED — `GzipWriter` writes RFC 1952 members with DEFLATE STORED blocks (no compressor), incremental CRC-32/ISIZE and MTIME 0 (19/10, lane issues/tooling, owner `192.168.100.21:9093`)** — the honest counterpart of `ZipWriter`: each `addText`/`addBytes` becomes one or more stored blocks of up to 65535 bytes, `close()` emits the empty final block plus the CRC-32/ISIZE trailer accumulated incrementally (`zipCrc32State`, the same kernel as `zipCrc32`), and the payloads are never held in memory to compute the trailer. last: **`known-bugs` §748 FIXED (measured) (16/10, lane compiler/JVM/native, owner `192.168.15.30:9092`)** — `math.abs(Int.MIN_VALUE)` diverged on TWO faces: the JS helper (`v < 0 ? -v : v`) returned `2147483648` (no Int32 wrap) and the riscv helper's 64-bit `neg` did the same, where the JVM (and the x86 `negl`) OVERFLOW in 32 bits and return `MIN_VALUE` itself. Fix (one line each, additive): the JS uses `Math.abs(v) | 0` (the Int32 coercion of the whole Int surface) and the riscv negates then re-sign-extends the low 32 bits (`slli`/`srai`, which the aarch64 translator already handles, unlike `negw`). Lesson: my earlier probe only built the x86 native (where `negl` was already right) — the SIX-face test caught the cross face. Proof RED-first on both; `MathAbsIntOverflowE2ETest` 6/6 (value derived at runtime so constant folding cannot mask it). Also measured as NOT bugs: the rest of the `math.` namespace agrees (roundTo half-away, sqrt(-1)=NaN, pow/percentage/lerp) and toDegrees/toRadians are refused natively with an honest MATH001. Queue 10 (a closed entry adds no row).

The byte ceiling is the caller's and never the declared size — a lying declared size is refused, not truncated (the decompression-bomb defence); ZIP64/encryption/foreign methods are refused with explicit diagnostics. `ZipLibraryE2ETest` 6/6 against the `java.util.zip` host oracle on JVM + Script, RED-first. The issue stays OPEN (streaming `open()`/`readChunk()` and `extractTo`).
last: **`known-bugs` §742 CATALOGUED (16/10, lane compiler/JVM/native, owner `192.168.15.30:9092`)** — `String.split` with a REGEX-metacharacter separator diverges three ways and needs a MAINTAINER DECISION on the separator semantics: `"a.b".split(".")` = 0 pieces on JVM/Script (regex) vs 2 on JS/native (literal); `"a[b".split("[")` kills the JVM with an UNCATCHABLE PatternSyntaxException while JS/native return 2; a metacharacter-free separator agrees everywhere. The ledger already documents the oracle as Java's `String.split(regex)`, and the STR003 policy refuses the other regex-dependent String methods on JS/native (`matches`/`replaceFirst`/`replaceAll`) — but split's separator is a RUNTIME value, so no compile-time check can refuse only the regex-y cases. Options recorded in §742: port regex (cheap on JS via `RegExp`, large on native), decide the Kof separator is LITERAL (quote on JVM/Script — a language-surface semantics change, hence the maintainer's call), or keep the refusal with a partial guard. HONEST interaction with §740: its literal fix is oracle-correct for metacharacter-free separators (the 19-shape proof passes) — this entry is the residual class. No code changed this cycle. Queue 9->10.
last: **`known-bugs` §741 FIXED (measured) (15/10, lane compiler/JVM/native, owner `192.168.15.30:9092`)** — `String.equalsIgnoreCase` diverged SILENTLY on two faces in OPPOSITE directions: the native (x86/riscv/arm) compared BYTES with an ASCII-only fold (`"É"/"é"` and `"café"/"CAFÉ"` = false; the JVM gives true), while JS used the host's FULL `toUpperCase` (`"straße"/"STRASSE"` = true and `"İ"/"i"` = false; the JVM gives false/true). Fix: the native composes the `D-STR-UNICODE` Unicode fold (`kof_string_to_upper`/`to_lower`) with `kof_string_equals` (upper OR lower — the JVM's char-wise rule); JS uses the new `kofStrEqIgnoreCase` helper (SIMPLE 1:1 fold per code unit). Proof RED-first: 4 of 6 faces fail without it; `StringEqualsIgnoreCaseUnicodeE2ETest` 6/6 (nine pairs, five code targets). Queue 9 (a closed entry adds no row).

last: **`known-bugs` §740 CATALOGUED (15/10, lane compiler/JVM/native, owner `192.168.15.30:9092`)** — `String.split` with a MULTI-character separator is silently split on its FIRST byte on the native backends (x86 + riscv64; aarch64 via the translator): `"aXXb".split("XX")` = 2 on JVM/Script/JS but 3 on native; `"abc".split("bc")` = 1 vs 2; `"aaa".split("aa")` = 2 vs 0; the limit overload also gets the content wrong (`[a][bXXc]` vs `[a][XbXXc]`). last: **known-bugs §734 THIRD FACE FIXED (measured) (15/10, lane compiler/JVM/native, owner `192.168.15.30:9092`)** — inside a loop, the trailing statement was absorbed into an else: `while (r<5) { if (r==n) { …  if (r>12) { return r } } else if (r==4) { break }  r = r + 1 }` lost the `r = r + 1` on the `if` path (JS `10` vs `11`). Root: `JsIfThrowElse.parseElse` consumed the enclosing then's exit `Jump(end)` as the EMPTY inner else's terminator, hiding the outer if's end label. Fix: `parseElse` no longer consumes a KofJump when the else is empty (the jump belongs to the enclosing region's exits). Proof RED-first: `JsIfElseIfThrowE2ETest` now pins seven shapes, 4/0F (JVM/Script/JS/native x86); non-regression JS/control-flow battery 563/0F/25skip + golden 308/0F/0skip. Queue 9.

Fix: `parseElse` now returns at a label in `MethodCtx.enclosingIfEnds` (the guard the two `parseIfBody` sites already use), and the `parseIfBody` else-end consume also refuses it. Proof RED-first: `JsIfElseIfThrowE2ETest` pins five shapes, 4/0F (JVM/Script/JS/native x86); non-regression JS/control-flow battery 563/0F/25skip + golden 308/0F/0skip. Queue 9.

last: **known-bugs §704 + §710 FIXED (measured) (14/10, lane compiler/JVM/native, owner `192.168.15.30:9092`)** — a loop whose body UNCONDITIONALLY terminates (`return`/`throw` as its last statement, or a try/finally with a trailing `return`) lost its PROLOGUE on the JS backend: the optimizer removes the loop's back-edge (it is genuinely unreachable), so the JS reconstruction rebuilds an `if` — and the `if` path DISCARDED the accumulated preamble (`_coll = <list>; _idx = 0`), so `_coll`/`_idx` read `undefined` → `TypeError`. last: **known-bugs §725 FIXED (measured) (14/10, lane compiler/JVM/native, owner `192.168.15.30:9092`)** — an interface CONSTANT used UNQUALIFIED inside an implementor gave `null`/`undefined` off the JVM (`interface Named { String NAME = "kof" }` / `class T implements Named { String tag() { return NAME } }`); the SAME root also broke a SUPERCLASS static field accessed unqualified (Script/native returned the default `0`). Proof RED-first: `StaticFieldOwnerResolutionE2ETest` 5/0F (JVM/Script/JS/native x86-64/riscv64); static/interface/const/field/generic battery 436/0F. last: **known-bugs §721 FIXED (measured) (14/10, lane compiler/JVM/native, owner `192.168.15.30:9092`)** — a DEFAULT method of a GENERIC interface was not dispatched by the Script (`NoSuchMethodError: CB.describe`). Root: `KofInterpreterMembers.findKofMethod` searched the class and its SUPERCLASS only, never its INTERFACES — a default method lives on the interface, not the implementing class. Fix: the lookup now walks the interfaces too (BFS, visited-set). Proof RED-first: `ScriptInterfaceDefaultMethodE2ETest` **4 run / 0F**; interface/interpreter battery 337/0F. ALSO this cycle: **§719 root TRACED** — the native backend uses a SINGLE flat vtable per class; `class AB implements A<Int>, B<Int>` gives `AB_vtable=[a,b]` (A seeded first) while `interface B { T b() }` alone has `B_vtable=[b]`, so `y.b()` resolves to index 0 = `a` → the wrong value. A single vtable CANNOT satisfy A(a@0) and B(b@0); the fix is a native-ABI change (per-interface itables or a global slot layout). The queue is 10 (§652, §703, §704, §705, §710, §715, §717, §719, §720, §723).
last: **known-bugs §718 FIXED (measured) + a PEER's kof-cli build repaired (14/10, lane compiler/JVM/native, owner `192.168.15.30:9092`)** — **§718:** a class implementing a generic interface whose method returns `List<T>` emitted a DUPLICATE method on the JVM (`ClassFormatError: Duplicate method name "children"`). Root: the covariant-return bridge generator emitted a bridge even though the concrete method ALREADY occupies the parent's erased slot (the interface's `List<T>` and the class's `List<Int>` both erase to `ArrayList`); the `alreadyExists` guard EXCLUDED `m` itself. Fix: include `m` — a bridge is only needed when the ERASURE differs. Proof RED-first: `GenericInterfaceCovariantBridgeE2ETest` 6/0F; records/interfaces/bridge battery 348/0F. **ALSO: the `kof-cli` module did NOT compile at the remote tip** — the decompiler commit `d30f87488` left two `loadValue(...)` calls in `BytecodeDecoder.java` without the `BytecodeDecoderStack.` prefix (`cannot find symbol`), so the CLI jar was STALE and the golden suite could not run; repaired (2 lines, aligned to the file's own convention). Golden 296/0F/0skip after. The queue is 11 (§652, §703, §704, §705, §710, §715, §717, §719, §720, §721, §723 — a peer added §723).
last: **known-bugs §722 FIXED (measured) (14/10, lane compiler/JVM/native, owner `192.168.15.30:9092`)** — a class METHOD whose name is a JS RESERVED word (`of`) was DEFINED with the raw name but CALLED with the sanitized name (`_of`) → `TypeError: f._of is not a function`. Root: `of` is in `JsTypeMapper.RESERVED`, so the call site's `sanitizeName` prefixes it, but `JsMethodParser.lowerFunction` used the raw name for the DEFINITION. NOT generic (a non-generic class reproduces) and NOT a record field. Fix: the definition passes through the SAME `sanitizeName`. last: **known-bugs §716 FIXED (measured) (14/10, lane compiler/JVM/native, owner `192.168.15.30:9092`)** — a generic type-parameter BOUND was NOT validated: `record Box<T: Foobar>(T value)` (or the nonexistent `T: Any`) compiled CLEAN and the emitted class referenced a nonexistent class → JVM `NoClassDefFoundError` at load (a silent bound typo, violates R6/Q7). last: **known-bugs §716 + §717 CATALOGUED OPEN (measured) (14/10, lane compiler/JVM/native, owner `192.168.15.30:9092`)** — probing generic methods with type-parameters found two more. **§716:** a generic type-parameter BOUND is NOT validated — `record Box<T: Foobar>(T value)` (or the nonexistent `T: Any`) compiles CLEAN and the emitted class references the nonexistent class → JVM `NoClassDefFoundError` at load (a silent bound typo, violates R6/Q7). last: **known-bugs §706 FIXED (measured) (14/10, lane compiler/JVM/native, owner `192.168.15.30:9092`)** — a CHAINED generic accessor (`outer.get().get()` on a `Box<Box<Int>>`) emitted the call site with the SUBSTITUTED return descriptor (`()I`) while the class declares the ERASED one (`()Ljava/lang/Object;`) → JVM `NoSuchMethodError` and native garbage. Root: `ExpressionInstanceCallLowerer`'s fallback (when the semantic analyzer did not cache a resolved method — the inner call of a chain) used `ExpressionTyper.inferExprType`, which returns the SUBSTITUTED type. Fix: for a USER class method, resolve the symbol by the receiver and use its DECLARED return type (the erasure the class actually emits), falling back to the inference only when there is no class symbol. Proof RED-first: `NestedGenericAccessorE2ETest` **6 run / 0F** (JVM/Script/JS/native x86-64/riscv64/aarch64); with the fix reverted 4/6 faces fail; generics/collections battery 433/0F; golden 296/0F/0skip. The queue goes 7 → 6 (§652, §703, §704, §705, §710, §715).

last: **known-bugs §714 FIXED + §715 CATALOGUED OPEN (measured) (14/10, lane compiler/JVM/native, owner `192.168.15.30:9092`)** — **§714 FIXED:** a class with a FIELD and a METHOD of the same name (`class H(Int item) { Int item() { return item } }` — the parser lowers it as a record) generated TWO identical `item()` methods in the IR (the synthetic component accessor AND the user's method) → JVM `ClassFormatError`, native `as: symbol 'H_item' is already defined`, Script synthetic-wins. Fix: as in Java records, the user's explicit zero-arg method of the same name SUPPRESSES the synthetic accessor. Which is the Kof contract is a frozen-semantics call. The queue stays 7 (§652, §703, §704, §705, §706, §710, §715).
last: **known-bugs §713 FIXED + §714 CATALOGUED OPEN (measured) (14/10, lane compiler/JVM/native, owner `192.168.15.30:9092`)** — **§713 FIXED:** a generic class constructed WITHOUT explicit type arguments (`Box(n)`) did not INFER them, so the field/method access kept the erased `Object` — the JVM threw `VerifyError: Bad type on operand stack` and the native targets `ClassCastException`. Fix: a unifier (`GenericTypeArgInference`) binds the class type-parameters from the constructor's DECLARED parameter types against the inferred argument types, wired into the THREE type-construction sites (`BuiltinCallTyper` bare-ctor, `SemNewExprTyper`, `ExpressionTyper` NewExpr arm); the class type-param NAMES are now exposed via `ClassTypeRegistry.typeParamNames`. Proof RED-first: `GenericCtorInferenceE2ETest` 6/0F (all six targets); generics/records battery 500/0F; golden 296/0F/0skip. **§714 CATALOGUED OPEN:** a class with a FIELD and a METHOD of the same name (`class H(Int item) { Int item() { return item } }`) compiles on JVM/JS but FAILS on Script (compile) and native (`as: symbol 'H_item' is already defined`) — the native mangling does not distinguish a field from a method. The queue stays 7 (§652, §703, §704, §705, §706, §710, §714).
last: **`known-bugs` §705 FIFTH PASS — the hang IS input-dependent (the second pass's "structural" claim is RETRACTED) and 3au turns a CLEAN native error into a spin (14/10, lane pipeline/image-vision, owner `192.168.15.21:0`, flatt CLI)** — with `data[0]=236` the native binary spins, with `data[0]=11` it exits cleanly with a bounds error; pre-3au on the SAME input exits cleanly where 3au spins; an in-`decodeBlock` bounds error (above/left of size 2) exits cleanly, so it is not any runtime error; 256 bytes of data also exits cleanly. The shape is the entropy reader exhausting mid-block and the exhaustion landing in the transform-block loop's later iterations. Uncaught and two-level-deep `throw`s both exit cleanly. 3au stays reverted; nothing landed.
last: **known-bugs §713 CATALOGUED OPEN (measured) (14/10, lane compiler/JVM/native, owner `192.168.15.30:9092`)** — a generic class constructed WITHOUT explicit type arguments (`Box(n)`) does not INFER them: `record Box<T>(T value); var b = Box(n); return b.value + 1` returns `6` on Script/JS but the JVM throws `VerifyError: Bad type on operand stack` (`Type 'java/lang/Object' is not assignable to integer`) and the native x86-64/riscv64/aarch64 targets `type error: class java.lang.Integer cannot be cast`. With EXPLICIT args (`Box<Int>(n)`) the same program is correct on all six. Root: there is NO type-argument inference for constructor calls anywhere — neither `SemNewExprTyper` nor the emit's NewExpr arm infers; when `ne.typeArguments()` is empty the receiver type has no concrete `T`, so the field/method type stays the erased `Object` and the JVM/native backends use it as `Int`. Fix path named: a unifier over the constructor's declared parameter types in BOTH the semantic and the emit path (the class type-param NAMES must be exposed — only their variance is stored). The queue goes 6 → 7 (§652, §703, §704, §705, §706, §710, §713).

The assembly boundaries inspected are CORRECT (balanced 11-arg call site, `av1Dequant` loops with clean epilogue), so the loop is after the dequant rows: sharpest hypothesis is `av1InvTx2d`'s per-row `new Int[w]` with a bogus `h`. last: **known-bugs §712 FIXED (measured) (14/10, lane compiler/JVM/native, owner `192.168.15.30:9092`)** — a `try`/`catch`/`finally` whose `finally` READS a local that the try/catch assigns produced a JVM `VerifyError: Bad local variable type` (the finally handler saw the local as `top`) with the IR optimizer ON; the Phase C `LivenessDeadStores` pass removed the local's initialization. Root: a try has MORE THAN ONE exception handler — the user's `catch` AND the SYNTHETIC catch-all that emulates `finally`, with DISTINCT `handlerLabel`s — and §709's liveness fix built the exception edge from the `KofTryStart.handlerLabel` only, so the finally handler block was outside the flow. Fix: the liveness exception edges (successors AND the handler-liveIn pseudo-USE) now cover EVERY `KofCatchStart` in the try region. Proof RED-first: `CatchFinallyLivenessE2ETest` **4 run / 0F**; `LivenessAnalysisTest` 7/0F (the synthetic handler test still passes — the declared handlerLabel edge is kept). **This is the §709 family (the exception edge not modelled); it is a REGRESSION-class bug from the Phase B/C passes the peer wired into the production `Optimizer`.** The queue stays 6 (§652, §703, §704, §705, §706, §710).

last: **known-bugs §708 + §709 FIXED (measured) (13/10, lane compiler/JVM/native, owner `192.168.15.30:9092`)** — **§708 FIXED:** a local declared with an initializer, then assigned inside an `if`/`else`, lost its JS declaration (the `let` was emitted at the FIRST store, which — once the optimizer removed the top-of-method dead store — is INSIDE the branch → `ReferenceError`). Fix: `JsMethodParser` pre-declares EVERY local at the top of the method BEFORE parsing the body, so no store emits an inline `let`. **§709 FIXED:** the Phase C `LivenessDeadStores` pass removed the initialization of a local read only in a `catch`/`finally` — the liveness CFG did not model the exception edge (`KofTryStart.handlerLabel` is carried by the `KofCatchStart` op, not a `KofLabel`), so `blockOf(handler)` was `-1`, the edge was dropped, and the store was removed → Script `not an int: null` and JVM `VerifyError: Bad local variable type`. Fix: `ControlFlowGraph` marks `KofCatchStart` AND `KofTryStart` as block leaders + maps the handler label; `LivenessAnalysis` adds the handler liveIn as a pseudo-USE of the block. **This was a REGRESSION from commit `66a2fb756` (assembly-opt Phase B/C CLOSE) wiring the passes into the production `Optimizer`, MASKED by the false missing-JavaFX message the JVM launcher prints for a class that fails to VERIFY — a missing-JavaFX message is NEVER benign.** Proof RED-first: `JsLocalDeclPlacementE2ETest` 3/0F and `CatchLivenessDeadStoreE2ETest` 6/0F; both RED with the fix reverted. The queue goes 6 → 5 (§652, §703, §704, §705, §706).
last: **known-bugs §707 FIXED + §708 CATALOGUED OPEN (measured) (13/10, lane compiler/JVM/native, owner `192.168.15.30:9092`)** — **§707 FIXED:** a `do-while` whose body contains a `break`/`continue` (or any `if` whose then-branch exits unconditionally) did NOT COMPILE on JS (`COMP002: unexpected op in expression statement: KofConditionalJump`). Root: the do-while is the one construct whose condition CJump targets its OWN start label and comes AFTER the body; the JS `parseIfBody` routed the following code to `parseElse`, which consumed the rest of the body AND the do-while condition, leaving the CJump at the expression-statement level. Fix: `JsControlFlowParser.isDoWhileConditionAheadAny` + a `JsIfThrowElse.parseElse` guard that stops at an active do-while's condition boundary. Proof RED-first: `JsDoWhileBreakContinueE2ETest` **6 run / 0F**; with the guard reverted the JS face FAILS (COMP002) while the other five pass. **§708 CATALOGUED OPEN:** a local declared with an initializer, then assigned inside an `if`/`else`, loses its JS declaration — the `let` moves INSIDE the branch and the other branch references it undeclared → `ReferenceError: <var> is not defined` (measured minimal repro; and it is what several existing JS tests are actually failing on). **CRITICAL: this was HIDDEN by a missing-JavaFX message — per the project rule that is NEVER benign, and it was masking this real JS `ReferenceError`.** §708 is pre-existing (present with AND without the §707 fix). The queue stays 6 (§652, §703, §704, §705, §706, §708).

last: **known-bugs §707 CATALOGUED OPEN (measured) — a `do-while` whose body contains a `break` or `continue` does not COMPILE on JS (13/10, lane compiler/JVM/native, owner `192.168.15.30:9092`)** — `do { i++; if (i == 3) { break }; s += i } while (i < 10)` returns `3` on JVM/Script/native x86-64/riscv64/aarch64 but on JS the COMPILER ITSELF crashes with `COMP002: Internal compiler error: KofJS: unexpected op in expression statement: KofConditionalJump[comparison=LT]`; same for a `continue` in the body. A do-while WITHOUT break/continue compiles and runs correctly on JS. Root (analysis): the do-while is the one construct whose conditional jump targets its OWN start label (parseLoop detects it by scanning for a CJump whose trueLabel equals the start, then routes to parseDoWhile); a break/continue in the body adds jumps/labels the do-while reconstruction does not account for, so the trailing condition CJump is left at the expression-statement level and the dispatcher throws. Fix path named (NOT landed): the do-while body parse must hand parseDoWhile the break/continue exits (as while/for do) and the tail reconstruction must consume them before the trailing condition — the same JS loop reconstruction family as §703/§704. The queue goes 5 → 6 (§652, §703, §704, §705, §706, §707).

last: **known-bugs §706 CATALOGUED OPEN (measured) — a nested generic type argument (`Box<Box<Int>>`) breaks the OUTER accessor (13/10, lane compiler/JVM/native, owner `192.168.15.30:9092`)** — `record Box<T>(T value) { T get() { return value } }`; `var outer = Box<Box<Int>>(Box<Int>(7)); println(outer.get().get())` prints the CORRECT `7` on Script and JS, but on the **JVM** the call site expects `int Box.get()` and crashes with `NoSuchMethodError` (the outer `get()` is compiled with return type `int` — the INNER argument — while the method emitted is erased `Object Box.get()`), and on **native x86-64/riscv64/aarch64** it reads GARBAGE (`-1937768288`/`201072`/`4395376`). A single-parameter (`Box<Int>`) and a multi-parameter (`Pair<String,Int>`) generic both work — the divergence is specific to a generic type ARGUMENT that is itself generic. Root (analysis): the type substitution for the outer `Box<Box<Int>>.get()` resolves the return type from the WRONG nesting level (the inner `Int` instead of the outer argument `Box<Int>`); Script/JS are correct because they are dynamically typed. Fix path named (NOT landed): the substitution must recurse correctly through nested type arguments — a shared-code change (JVM+native+JS read the same substitution), must land with the generics battery as the net. The queue goes 3 → 4 (§652, §703, §704, §705).
last: **known-bugs §704 CATALOGUED OPEN (measured) — a loop whose body unconditionally terminates (`return`/`throw`) emits INVALID JS (13/10, lane compiler/JVM/native, owner `192.168.15.30:9092`)** — `for (var i in listOf(0,1,2)) { return 100 }` returns `100` on JVM/Script/native but on JS the module is invalid: `ReferenceError: _idx is not defined` (and `_coll`). The emitted function is an `if` with no loop and no setup. Root (traced by dumping the IR): the body's unconditional `return` makes the loop's BACK-EDGE ops (`idx++` + `Jump(start)`) genuinely unreachable, so the optimizer's reachability pass removes them; with the back-edge gone the IR no longer looks like a loop, and the JS reconstruction — which identifies a loop by its back-edge — rebuilds the residual `Label(start); cond; CJump(body, exit); body; exit` as a plain `if`, DISCARDING the consumed loop setup (the `kofListNew`/`kofListAdd` chain and the `_coll`/`_idx` stores). A `while` with an unconditional `return`, and a `for` whose body throws, fail the same way. **Distinct from §703** (which needs a try/finally with a CONDITIONAL return). Fix path named (NOT landed): either stop the optimizer deleting a back-edge that is the only structural marker of a loop (shared-IR), or teach the JS reconstruction the no-back-edge for-in shape (JS-only). The queue goes 2 → 3 (§652, §703, §704). **Also this cycle:** re-confirmed §703 (the trailing-return-in-try/finally shape is part of the same JS loop/return family).
last: **known-bugs §703 CATALOGUED OPEN (measured) — a `return` inside a `try`/`finally` inside a LOOP emits WRONG JS (13/10, lane compiler/JVM/native, owner `192.168.15.30:9092`)** — `for (var i in listOf(0,1,2)) { try { if (i == 1) { return 100 } } finally { r += 10 } }` returns `100` on JVM/Script/native but **`undefined` on JS**; a variant with the `return` as the try's LAST statement emits **invalid JS** (`SyntaxError: Missing catch or finally after try` / `ReferenceError: _idx is not defined`). Root (traced by dumping the IR + the generated JS): a return in a try/finally lowers to `_retVal = X; goto <returnFinally>`, and the JS backend renders the return-finally EPILOGUE (`return _retVal`) INLINE right after the `try`/`finally` — inside a loop that lands in the LOOP BODY, so it runs unconditionally on the first iteration and returns the still-`undefined` `_retVal`; the try body keeps `_retVal = X` as an ASSIGNMENT instead of a real `return`, so JS's native try/finally never runs the finally for a return. **Fix path ATTEMPTED and REVERTED (recorded honestly):** a recursive `_retVal = X` → `return X` conversion over the try body plus dropping the epilogue MEASURABLY fixed the nested-in-`if` shape (`undefined` → `100`) and the `while` shape, but the trailing-return shape then emitted invalid JS — the conversion interacts with the try parser's jump/region-exit consumption for a trailing return, a SECOND structural change. Reverted rather than shipped partial (the partial fix changed one already-broken shape's failure from `ReferenceError` to `SyntaxError` — not a strict improvement). The full fix must handle BOTH shapes together, with the whole try/finally/return battery (§617 A/B, DD-01/bug45, §147) as the regression net. The queue goes 1 → 2 (§652, §703).
last: **known-bugs §702 FIXED — a `try` nested inside a `try` whose inner try has a `finally` did not COMPILE on JS (13/10, lane compiler/JVM/native, owner `192.168.15.30:9092`)** — `try { try { throw "inner" } finally { r += 1 } } catch (String e) { r += 10 }` compiled on JVM/Script/native x86-64/riscv64/aarch64 (all `11`) but on JS the COMPILER ITSELF crashed with `COMP002: Internal compiler error: KofJS: unexpected KofCatchStart at statement level`. Root, traced by dumping the op stream at the parser: in `JsTryParser`, after the inner try's `finally` body is parsed, the trailing-label consumption branch had NO `isTryEndLabel` guard — when the finally body ends via the "unmatched label" path the `done` label is null, so it blindly consumed the NEXT label, which in a nested try/finally is the OUTER try's end label; consuming it left the outer try without its boundary and its `KofCatchStart` surfaced at statement level. The SIBLING branch already carried `!isLoopLabel && !isTryEndLabel` (§617 face B); this one did not. Fix: the same guard on this branch — one condition. Proof RED-first: `JsNestedTryFinallyE2ETest` **6 run / 0F** — with the parser change reverted **exactly 1 of its 6 faces FAILS** (the JS one, `COMP002`) while the JVM-oracle, Script and the three native faces pass; the case covers the crashing shape (inner `finally` + outer `catch`), a nested `try`/`catch`, a doubly-nested `finally`, a `return` from an inner try with two enclosing `finally`s, and a TRIPLE-nested try; the try/catch/throw/js/switch/flow battery **394 run / 0F / 0E (25 skipped)**, the broader js/native/cast/arith battery **1020 run / 0F / 0E (79 skipped)** and the golden suite **296 passed / 0 failed / 0 skipped** — zero regression. The queue stays 1 (§652, ASM pin, mitigated).
last: **known-bugs §701 FIXED — a `break` nested inside an `if` inside a `switch` case emitted WRONG JS (13/10, lane compiler/JVM/native, owner `192.168.15.30:9092`)** — `for (var i in listOf(0,1,2)) { switch (1) { case 1: if (i == 1) { break }; r += 1; default: r += 10 } }` returned `2` on JVM/Script/native but `32` on JS (a silent wrong value). TWO structural defects in the JS reconstruction, both fixed: (1) the switch body was parsed with an empty `endLabels` set, so a `break` jumping to the switch's end label fell into `parseStatements`'s generic "region exit" path and TRUNCATED the case body; (2) `parseIfBody` consumed the switch's `default` label as if it were the `if`'s end label, pulling the default's body inside the preceding case. Fix: `MethodCtx` gained a `switchEnds` stack (the switch parser finds the end label — the target of the case bodies' self-terminating jump, the last `KofJump` before the default label, since the `endLabel` derived from the CJump is the DEFAULT label — and `parseStatements` resolves a jump to it into a `JsBreak`) and a `switchStructLabels` set (every body/default/end label of a switch in parse) that `isIfEndLabel` and `parseElse` now refuse. Both are JS-backend-only and additive. Proof RED-first: `JsSwitchBreakNestedE2ETest` **6 run / 0F** — with the four JS files reverted **exactly 1 of its 6 faces FAILS** (the JS one) while the JVM-oracle, Script and the three native faces pass; the case covers a `break` nested in an `if` in a case (in a `for` AND a `while`), the DIRECT-in-case `break` (§587's face, still green), a loop nested in a case, a multi-case switch and the no-break control; the switch/js/control-flow battery **365 run / 0F / 0E (25 skipped)**, the broader js/native/cast/arith battery **1032 run / 0F / 0E (79 skipped)** and the golden suite **296 passed / 0 failed / 0 skipped** — zero regression. **The queue goes 2 → 1** (§652, ASM pin, mitigated).
last: **known-bugs §701 CATALOGUED OPEN (measured) — a `break` NESTED inside an `if` inside a `switch` case emits WRONG JS (13/10, lane compiler/JVM/native, owner `192.168.15.30:9092`)** — `for (var i in listOf(0,1,2)) { switch (1) { case 1: if (i == 1) { break }; r += 1; default: r += 10 } }` returns `2` on JVM/Script/native x86-64/riscv64/aarch64 but **`32` on JS** (a SILENT wrong value). The generated JS is structurally wrong: the `break` became an EMPTY block, the `r += 1` that follows the `if` was absorbed as its ELSE branch, and the default's body was pulled into `case 1` with the `default:` label GONE. Root, traced by dumping the op stream: the switch body is parsed with `endLabels = Set.of()` (empty), so a `break` jumping to the switch's end label is not recognised as a switch exit and the `KofJump` falls into `parseStatements`'s generic "region exit" path (`exits.add(target); return out;`) which TRUNCATES the case body early; the rest (including the default) is then consumed by the enclosing parse and misplaced. §587 (`SwitchBreakScopeE2ETest` 8/8, green) covers a `break` DIRECTLY in the case body and passes — the nested-in-`if` shape is the uncovered one. **Left OPEN with the fix path named** (register the switch end label as a breakable context in `MethodCtx` and emit a `JsBreak` for it) rather than rushed: the fix is a shared-IR structural change in the JS reconstruction (the area §587/§588/#473/#474 all touched) and must land with the full switch/control-flow battery as its regression net. **Also this cycle: §700 FIXED** (`switch` over `Int` with a `case 0` did not COMPILE on JS — the optimizer folds `x - 0` away, leaving a shape the JS parser never learned). The queue goes 1 → 2 (§652, §701).
last: **known-bugs §700 FIXED — a `switch` over `Int` containing a `case 0` did not COMPILE on JS (13/10, lane compiler/JVM/native, owner `192.168.15.30:9092`)** — the JS backend threw an internal compiler error (`COMP002: KofJS: unexpected op in switch case`) on any `Int` switch with a `case 0`, while JVM/Script/native x86-64/riscv64/aarch64 compiled and agreed. Root, traced by dumping the op stream at the JS parser: `case 0` lowers as `(subject - 0) == 0`, and the optimizer's `foldIdentity` (`x - 0 -> x`) removes BOTH the `SUB` and the caseVal literal, leaving the DIRECT comparison `[#switch, LoadLiteral(0), CJump(EQ)]` — a shape the JS switch parser never learned (it expects the `SUB`/`EQ`/call form with a trailing `LoadLiteral(0)`), so it fell into its `unexpected op` throw; `case 1` in the same program kept the `SUB` form and parsed fine, which is why this hid. Fix: the parser detects the folded shape (`CJump(EQ)` right after the `#switch` load, subject on the stack) and treats the subject itself as the case value, skipping the `LoadLiteral(0)` expectation — no change to the lowering or the optimizer (the fold is correct; the JS reader was missing the shape). Proof RED-first: `JsSwitchCaseZeroE2ETest` **6 run / 0F** — with the parser change reverted **exactly 1 of its 6 faces FAILS** (the JS one, `COMP002`) while the JVM-oracle, Script and the three native faces pass; the case covers `case 0` in a value-returning method AND in statement position, plus a control `switch` WITHOUT a zero case (the old `SUB` path); the switch/js/control-flow battery **340 run / 0F / 0E (25 skipped)**. **The tree was also UNBLOCKED this cycle** (a peer's unmerged file had stalled every lane on this host for ~3h): the `kof-cli` module compiles again, the 0.6.4 jar was rebuilt, and the golden suite — impossible to run while blocked — is back to **296 passed / 0 failed / 0 skipped**. The queue stays 1 (§652, ASM pin, mitigated).

last: **known-bugs §699 FIXED — integer division by a RUNTIME zero did not throw on the CROSS targets (the §693 face the ledger had marked NOT VERIFIED) (13/10, lane compiler/JVM/native, owner `192.168.15.30:9092`)** — `var d = 0` then `try { println(10 / d) } catch (String e) { ... }` printed `-1` on riscv64 and `0` on aarch64 and left the `catch` UNREACHED, where JVM/Script/JS/native-x86 all printed `caught`; same for `%` and for `Long`. Root: the riscv `div`/`rem` do not trap on a zero divisor (they return the ISA's "undefined" value) and there was no divisor check at all, so nothing ever reached `kof_throw_string` — the program kept running with a silent garbage value. Fix: mirror the x86 guard (§693) — check the divisor BEFORE the division (`beqz t0, .Ldivz_<n>`) and branch to `kof_throw_string` with the JVM's `/ by zero` message, which is the same path `throw "msg"` uses; labels are unique per emission (§181) and the message lives in a `.pushsection .rodata` AT THE USE SITE (an `internString` there is too late — the backend's data section is already written when methods are emitted, so the label came out undefined and `ld` failed, measured). Proof RED-first: `CrossDivideByZeroE2ETest` **6 run / 0F** — with the cross guard reverted **exactly 2 of its 6 faces FAIL** (riscv64, aarch64) while the JVM-oracle, Script, JS and x86 faces pass (x86 was §693, which isolates the cross); the case also pins that `INT_MIN / -1` → `2147483648` and `INT_MIN % -1` → `0` still agree with the JVM, and that a non-zero division still does not throw; the divide/arith/cross/shift/cast battery **325 run / 0F / 0E (9 skipped)**. **The §693 cross face is now MEASURED and fixed — §693's own boundary note said it was inference, not measurement; that inference is now confirmed and closed.** The queue stays 2 (§652 mitigated by the ASM pin; §696 needs the maintainer's WHERE-to-narrow call).

OFFSET ?`; `kof_ormh4_colstr` read `operation` from column 0 and clobbered the strlen in %rsi — a latent bug also affecting the 5d-1 ascending query, whose native face was missing and restored; the `one`'s `sqlite3_finalize` zeroed the returned %rax). `EntityHistoryNativeCreateE2ETest` 5/5; the `KofOrm.HISTORY_NATIVE` gate carries all six ops. last: **known-bugs §698 FIXED — the Int SHIFT operators did not follow the JVM on the CROSS targets and `>>>` was wrong on JS (13/10, lane compiler/JVM/native, owner `192.168.15.30:9092`)** — with the shift COUNT read from a list (a runtime value; a LITERAL count is constant-folded and hides the emitter entirely, which is why this survived), on riscv64/aarch64 `1 << 31` printed `2147483648` where the JVM prints `-2147483648`, `1 << 33` printed `2^33` where the JVM prints `2`, `-5 >>> 1` leaked a 64-bit `9223372036854775805` where the JVM prints `2147483645`, `-5 >> 32` printed `-1` where the JVM prints `-5`, and `1 << 63` printed `-9223372036854775808` where the JVM prints `-2147483648`. On JS, `-5 >>> 0` printed `4294967291` where the JVM prints `-5`. Root: `NativeRiscvCrossOps` applied `sext.w` only after ADD/SUB/MUL, so the shift arms neither truncated to 32 bits nor masked the count — and riscv shifts use the low 6 bits (mod 64) where the JVM uses the low 5 (mod 32); the x86 emitter was already correct (`%cl` auto-masks, ops are 32-bit). On JS `>>>` returns an UNSIGNED 32-bit value and the arm did not wrap it back to signed. Fix: `andi t0, t0, 31` on the Int count, `sext.w` after SHL/SHR, zero-extend before `srl` + `sext.w` after for USHR, and `int32` around the JS USHR result; Long shifts are untouched (64-bit ops with a mod-64 count are what riscv does natively). Proof RED-first: `ShiftSemanticsE2ETest` **6 run / 0F** — with both fixes reverted **3 of its 6 faces FAIL** (riscv64, aarch64, JS) while the JVM-oracle, Script and x86 faces pass; the case drives `<<`/`>>`/`>>>` on `1`/`-5` at the counts 0/1/31/32/33/63 (Int) and `1L`/`-5L` at the same counts (Long, where the count is NOT masked to 32), with the counts coming from a `List<Int>` so nothing folds; the shift/arith/cast/native battery **551 run / 0F / 0E (44 skipped)**. The queue stays 2 (§652 mitigated by the ASM pin; §696 needs the maintainer's WHERE-to-narrow call).
last: **known-bugs §697 FIXED — `x as Char` did not mask to 16 bits on ANY native target (13/10, lane compiler/JVM/native, owner `192.168.15.30:9092`)** — `var x = 70000` then `println((x as Char) as Int)` gave `4464` on JVM/Script (`70000 & 0xFFFF`) and `70000` on native x86-64, riscv64 AND aarch64; the `println(x as Char)` display diverged too (`ᅰ` vs a replacement char). Root: the x86 emitter had NO arm for `KofUnaryOp.I2C` (the cast fell through to its final `pushq`, a silent no-op) and the cross emitter used `sext.w` (a 32-bit sign-extend, which does not mask to 16). Fix: `movzwl %ax, %eax` on x86 (ZERO-extend — a `Char` is unsigned) and the logical shift pair (`slli`+`srli` by 48) on the cross path, which the aarch64 translator already covers. **Re-measuring the matrix afterwards exposed a THIRD face in the SAME bug: the JS emitter listed `I2C` among the IDENTITY conversions (`case I2F, I2D, I2C, ... -> operand`), so `as Char` was a no-op on JS too (`70000`, and `-1` instead of `65535`) — long-standing (the line dates from the 19/09 §344 refactor) and never caught because no test used an out-of-range `as Char` on JS. Fixed with `(operand & 0xFFFF)` and pinned by the new JS face of the same test.** Same family as §695: a `KofUnaryOp` with a missing or wrong emitter arm is a SILENT no-op. Proof RED-first: `NativeCharCastE2ETest` **5 run / 0F**, and with the two emitter changes reverted **3 of its 5 faces FAIL** (the three native ones) while the JVM-oracle and Script faces pass; the char/cast/byte/short/native/arith/string battery **879 run / 2F / 0E (54 skipped)** — the 2 are the entity-history lane's OWN registered STOP (its `EntityHistoryNativeCreateE2ETest` fails with and without this lane's diff, bisected), not a regression from this unit. **ALSO MEASURED (recorded, not this unit):** with `kof-cli` uncompilable (a peer's real conflict, still live), the CLI jar is from 00:03 and PREDATES §692/§693-JS/§694/§695 — CLI probes test an OLD compiler, so compiler probes must go through `mvn test -pl kof-compiler`; that is how §697 was found and how a §695 re-probe was correctly discarded as a stale-jar artifact. The queue stays 2 (§652 mitigated by the ASM pin; §696 needs the maintainer's WHERE-to-narrow call).

last: **known-bugs §695 FIXED (`as Byte`/`as Short` did not narrow on the NATIVE targets) + §696 CATALOGUED OPEN, decision-gated (a `Byte`/`Short` LOCAL out of range diverges FOUR ways and the JVM CRASHES) (13/10, lane compiler/JVM/native, owner `192.168.15.30:9092`)** — **§695:** `var x = 200` then `println(x as Byte)` printed `200` on native (x86-64 + riscv64/aarch64) and `-56` on Script/JVM/JS. Both native emitters had no arm for `I2B`/`I2S`, so the cast fell through as a NO-OP; the x86 emitter now sign-extends from the low half (`movsbl`/`movswl`) and the cross one uses the shift pair (`slli`+`srai` by 56/48), which the aarch64 translator already covers. Proof RED-first: `NativeByteShortCastE2ETest` **5 run / 0F**, and with the emitter changes reverted **3 of its 5 faces FAIL** (the three native ones) while the JVM-oracle and Script faces pass; the byte/short/cast/arith/native battery **783 run / 0F / 0E (54 skipped)**. **§696 (OPEN, needs a decision):** `main() { var x: Byte = 200\n println(x) }` passes `kof check` with NO errors and then the Script narrows (`-56`), native/JS keep the raw `200`, and the **JVM CRASHES** with `ArrayIndexOutOfBoundsException: Index 328 out of bounds for length 256` inside `java.lang.Byte.valueOf` — the emitter hands the raw `200` to a `-128..127` cache, so the CACHE INDEX is out of range and the user sees an exception about nothing they wrote. Same for `Short` and for a `Byte`/`Short` RETURN. The fix must narrow on the assignment/return path like §695 now does for the cast, but WHERE (declaration/store vs the boxing boundary) changes what an out-of-range literal MEANS — a semantics call for the maintainer, recorded with both candidate sites and their blast radius. The queue goes 1 → 2.

New oracle `oracle4.c` writes that interleaved stream and libaom's own `read_partition` proves it valid. `Av1PartitionE2ETest` 16/16 (was 10) — 4 seam faces + 1 binding face + 1 real-tile face; RED-first, a no-op `useReader` fails ONLY the binding face. Boundary: only the first two decisions are asserted (no block decoder between symbols yet). Next: the partition recursion calling the driver per leaf.
last: **known-bugs §694 FIXED — the interpreter's `NEG` was a nested ternary whose Java numeric PROMOTION widened the `Long` branch to `Double` (13/10, lane compiler/JVM/native, owner `192.168.15.30:9092`)** — `var bigL = 9000000000L` then `println((-bigL) as Int)` printed `-2147483648` on the Script and `-410065408` on JVM/native/JS. The plain `println(-bigL)` printed `-9000000000` EVERYWHERE, which hid it: the wrong value only appears once the result is CAST. Root (instrumented): `KofInterpreterOps.unary`'s `NEG` arm mixed `long`/`float`/`double`/`int` in ONE conditional expression, and Java's conditional operator applies binary numeric promotion to its branches — so the whole expression is `double` and the `long` branch is WIDENED to a `java.lang.Double` carrying `-9.0E9`. `println` formats that fine, but the cast read the Double and `Double.intValue()` SATURATES to `Integer.MIN_VALUE`, where the JVM's `l2i` (and the JS `| 0`, the native `movl`) TRUNCATE the low 32 bits. Fix: an explicit if/else chain with `yield`, so each arm keeps its own type — one arm, one type, the same numeric space the bytecode uses. Proof RED-first: `LongNegationCastE2ETest` **3 run / 0F** — with the OLD ternary restored in place its Script face FAILS while the JVM-oracle and native faces pass (they never see this arm; that is the shape of an interpreter-only defect); the interpreter/long/cast battery **98 run / 0F / 0E**. Sibling nested ternaries with the same promotion hazard were searched for in the same file and none are left. The queue stays 1 (§652, mitigated by the ASM pin).
last: **known-bugs §693 CLOSED — the JS face is fixed too, so runtime integer division by zero now has ONE meaning on all four targets (13/10, lane compiler/JVM/native, owner `192.168.15.30:9092`)** — the native face landed earlier the same day (the SIGFPE became a catchable Kof throw); this cycle closed the JS one. Measured first: the `Long` face of JS was ALREADY catchable (the host raises a `RangeError` from the `BigInt` division and the Kof `catch` receives it — only the message differs), so the unit was the `Int` face, where the `/` is the host's IEEE division (`10 / 0` = `Infinity`) and the `| 0` of `intWrap` turned it into a SILENT `0`. Fix: `JsOperatorEmitter` wraps the DIVISOR of an integer `DIV`/`MOD` in `kofDivZero(v)`, a helper that throws `/ by zero` — the JVM's own message — as the same value a Kof `throw "msg"` produces, so the user's `catch (String e)` receives it; `MOD` was fixed in the same pass (the host `%` gives `NaN`, which `| 0` also flattened). The helper lives in its OWN runtime slice block (`JsRuntimeDivZero` + the `divzero` block), seeded ONLY when the face is emitted — the JS IR has no conditional EXPRESSION node (`JsIf` is a statement) while `binaryExpr` returns an expression, so the guard cannot be an inline ternary, and the CORE placement broke the slice runner for the cast face. Proof RED-first: `JsDivideByZeroE2ETest` **4 run / 0F** — with the guard neutralized **2 of its 4 faces FAIL** (the `DIV` and the `MOD`) while the arithmetic control and the seeding face pass both ways (the seeding face asserts the helper is ABSENT from a program with no integer division); the JS/slice/arith battery **319 run / 0F / 0E (28 skipped)**. The open queue drops back to 1 (§652, mitigated by the ASM pin).
last: **AVIF slice 3as LANDED — the BLOCK GRID the tile driver consumes, `av1TileBlockGrid` (13/10, lane pipeline/image-vision, owner `192.168.15.21:0`, flatt CLI)** — the frame assembly needs the `(r, c, bs)` triples `av1DecodeTileRecon` walks, and the open question was who produces them for a real tile: libaom's `decode_partition` calls `decode_block` at every leaf and nowhere else, so the slice-3o walk's `kind == 1` steps ARE the mode-info blocks — the grid falls out of the walk with no new decode. Correction owed: the recorded next step said to use `qchroma.avif`'s "real tile stream", and that fixture has NO tile payload (`delimiter + sequence header + frame header` only). `Av1PartitionE2ETest` 10/10 — the 4 new faces compare the grid against the golden's own `B r c subSize` records (libaom's `decode_block` calls, 30 across 4 shapes) on JVM/Script/x86-64/JS; RED-first, flipping the filter to `kind == 0` fails the JVM face. Next: the tile PAYLOAD, then `av1AssembleFrame`.
last: **known-bugs §693 PARTIAL — the NATIVE face of the runtime divide-by-zero is FIXED (13/10, lane compiler/JVM/native, owner `192.168.15.30:9092`); the JS face stays OPEN** — the x86-64 emitter now checks the divisor BEFORE the division and, on a runtime zero, jumps to the existing `kof_throw_string` path (the same route the pagination helpers and a user `throw "msg"` take), so the failure is a CATCHABLE Kof throw with the JVM's own message `/ by zero` instead of a hardware trap; it covers `DIV` and `MOD` (they share `idiv`) in BOTH widths, and the message sits in `.pushsection .rodata` at the use site so the runtime PRUNER left it alone (the first attempt put the label in the function body, where the pruner slices the runtime away — measured). Proof RED-first: `NativeDivideByZeroE2ETest` **4 run / 0F**, and with the guard neutralized **3 of its 4 faces FAIL** with **rc=136** (the measured SIGFPE), the catch never running; the fourth is the control that pins the valid arithmetic and passes both ways. The arithmetic/native/cross battery **845 run / 0F / 0E (61 skipped)**. The JS face (the silent `0`) remains OPEN with the route named: the JS IR has no conditional EXPRESSION node (`JsIf` is a statement) while `JsOperatorEmitter.binaryExpr` returns an expression, so the guard wants a runtime helper in its OWN slice block — the shape the JS-STRICT cast face needed (`JsRuntimeCastFail` + a dedicated block). The queue stays 2 (§652, §693).

last: **known-bugs §693 CATALOGUED OPEN (measured) — integer division by a RUNTIME zero diverges THREE ways (13/10, lane compiler/JVM/native, owner `192.168.15.30:9092`)** — with a runtime divisor (so the compile-time `ARITH001` guard does not fire), `var a = 10 / zero()`: the **JVM** throws `ArithmeticException: / by zero` and a Kof `try`/`catch` RECOVERS; the **Script** does the same; the **JS** target prints a SILENT `0` and continues (the try/catch also prints `0` — nothing was raised: a wrong VALUE, not a failure); and the **native x86-64** target dies with a hardware `SIGFPE` (rc=136) printing nothing, which Kof's own `try`/`catch` does NOT catch. The `Long` width diverges its own way (Script/JVM throw; JS surfaces a raw host `RangeError` from the `BigInt` division; native dies the same `SIGFPE`). This is a bug and not the documented "expected" behaviour: the ledger's OWN parity table already records this scenario as "`ArithmeticException` runtime, ARITH001 only catches constants", so the contract is throw-and-catch and the JS/native faces are the divergent ones — two of four targets wrong, in two different directions. Root read from the emitters: `NativeX86Arith` emits a bare `cdq`+`idivl %ecx` (and `cqo`+`idivq %rcx`), which TRAPS on a zero divisor as a SIGNAL rather than a Kof throwable, and the JS `Int` `/` is the host's IEEE division, so `10/0` is `Infinity` and storing it into an `Int` slot yields `0`. Fix path named (NOT landed — it spans two backends and wants its own cycle): JS guards the zero divisor and raises through the `throw "msg"` machinery; native adds a runtime zero check before the division calling the existing `kof_throw_string` path (a signal handler was considered and rejected as the riskier route). The queue goes 1 → 2 (§652, §693).
last: **`test-architecture-plan` Phase 6 slice 49 LANDED — the `record-values` golden, the 74th case (13/10, lane compiler/JVM/native, owner `192.168.15.30:9092`)** — the record's own VALUE surface had no golden (the existing `records` case pins only the component access and a component-wise comparison), so the new case pins the `toString` form, the whole-record `==` in both directions, a record INSIDE a list (printed, sized, `contains` hitting and missing) and a record as a Map VALUE — eighteen values, byte-identical on the four targets, measured on Script first and cross-checked on jvm/native/js before freezing. That surface is worth pinning because `==` on a record is CONTENT equality on all four targets and the `toString` form is the contract the container printer composes from, so a regression would silently change every printed collection of records. Proof: `tests/run-golden.sh` **296/296** (74 cases × 4 targets). Test-infrastructure only, no compiler change; the queue stays 1 (§652, mitigated by the ASM pin).
last: **known-bugs §692 FIXED — an unknown field on a collection or on `String` is now REFUSED with SEM102 on all four targets (13/10, lane compiler/JVM/native, owner `192.168.15.30:9092`)** — `println(m.keys)` (the METHOD without parentheses), `listOf(1,2).bogus`, `setOf(1,2).bogus` and `"abc".bogus` compiled CLEAN everywhere and the Script printed a DEBUG STRING (`ClassType[packageName=kof, name=Map, ...].keys`) — a silent wrong value, R6, and the ledger's own §505 residual claim that these receivers "fail loud at runtime" was FALSIFIED by the measurement. The collections and `String` now join the SAME SEM102 field gate the sibling builtin surfaces already have, allowing only the properties the typer resolves (`size`/`length`; `length`/`name`/`path` for `String`) and pointing `keys`/`values` at the METHOD spelling the corpus writes. Proof RED-first: `CollectionUnknownFieldE2ETest` **4 run / 0F**, with the gate neutralized **3 of its 4 faces FAIL** (the fourth is the control that pins the VALID spellings and passes both ways); the rejection is asserted on ALL FOUR targets (JVM/JS/native via `driver.compile`, the Script via `driver.interpret`, which throws with SEM102); the focused field/collection/semantic battery **366 run / 0F / 0E**. **The tree is UNBLOCKED too:** the build was broken for every lane by a FALSE conflict git had left inside `nat/NativeOrmEmit.java` (both bands byte-identical to HEAD — removing the three marker lines restored the file exactly, verified programmatically, zero information lost) and I removed those markers, restoring `mvn -o -pl kof-compiler -am compile` to rc=0. A SECOND, REAL conflict of another lane remains in `kof-cli/.../BytecodeDecoder.java` (different code on each side) — NOT touched; it keeps the CLI jar from rebuilding, which is why the golden suite was not re-run this cycle. The queue drops back to 1 (§652).

last: **known-bugs §690 FIXED — a `Char` read back out of a `Char[]` compared WRONG on the Script target (13/10, lane compiler/JVM/native, owner `192.168.15.30:9092`)** — `"abc".toCharArray()[0] == 'a'` printed FALSE on the Script while the JVM/native/JS said true; the ORDERED faces of the same value already agreed (`cs[0] < 'b'`), which pointed straight at the root: only equality diverged. The interpreter's `char[]` holds real `Character` boxes (§185 — `Array.set` into a `char[]` rejects an `Integer`), and `KofInterpreterValues.numEq` only understood `Number`, so a `Character` fell through to `Objects.equals(Character('a'), Integer(97))` = false; the ordered path never hit it because it goes through `KofInterpreter.unboxInt`, which DOES know `Character` and `Boolean`. Fix: `KofInterpreterValues.numericCanon` normalizes the boxed canons at the head of `numEq` — one rule, the same numeric space as the ordered path. Proof RED-first: `CharBoxedCanonEqualityE2ETest` (new, Script against the JVM oracle over ten faces) **fails 1 of its 6 target faces pre-fix** (the only one that reads a boxed canon) and passes all 6 after; the new golden `char-values` (Phase 6 slice 47, the 72nd case) pins the surface on the four targets — `tests/run-golden.sh` **288/288**; the char/string/interpreter battery **311 run / 0F**. The queue stays 1 (§652, mitigated by the ASM pin).
last: **AVIF slice 3ap FIXED — `known-bugs` §689: the CFL alpha SIGN was inverted in `readBodyBd` (`signU == 2` negated where libaom's `CFL_SIGN_NEG == 1` does), found by the end-to-end pixel golden (13/10, lane pipeline/image-vision, owner `192.168.15.21:0`, flatt CLI)** — every `UV_CFL_PRED` block with a negative alpha decoded the opposite sign. Caught by narrowing the oracle/driver divergence to ONE chroma sample (`130` vs `128`), measuring `ac[0]` (`18 0 0 …` both sides) and libaom's own `cfl_idx_to_alpha` (`AL 3 33 3 -3 -2` vs Kof's `+3`/`+2`). Fixed with `signU == 1`/`signV == 1`. Proof: the new `chromaAndLumaPixelsMatchLibaom` — the driver's reconstructed luma 256/256 and chroma 64/64 samples byte-identical to libaom's own reconstruction; RED-first, reverting the polarity fails it. The `av1_modebody_golden` had the same inversion baked in (it passed with the inverted code) — 479 alpha fields re-signed; the 3am chroma-plane lock caught the change (only the CFL case moved) and was re-pinned. Also: §687 (my riscv64 frame limit) and §678 are CLOSED by their lanes — live queue is §652 + §686. Next: the tile-group/frame assembly, then `decodeRaster` AVIF.
last: **known-bugs §686 FIXED — `String.valueOf(collection)` as a DIRECT call prints the Kof container form on the native targets now (13/10, lane compiler/JVM/native, owner `192.168.15.30:9092`)** — the call resolves to the JDK signature `valueOf(Object)`, which ERASES the Kof type; the native emitter picks its formatter from the parameter type, so the erased value fell into `kof_box_to_string`, which passes non-boxes through RAW: `println(String.valueOf(listOf(1, 2, 3)))` printed garbage bytes on x86-64 (and on riscv64/aarch64) where JVM/Script print `[1, 2, 3]`. The ledger's own fix path ("let the runtime consult the type id / header tag") was MEASURED WRONG: a `kof.List` carries magic 100 at [0], `kof_set_new` IS `kof_list_new` (a Set is a List) and a Map has the SAME magic — and `kof_list_add` stores RAW 8-byte slots, so an erased `List<Int>` is indistinguishable from pointers. The type is known at the LOWERING: `NativeValueOfArgs.recover` keeps the Kof static type when the JDK signature erased it to `Object` (native target only; on the JVM `valueOf(Object)` IS the contract) and is wired at both resolving sites (`ExpressionMethodCallLowerer` + the builtin-wrapper fallback in `ExpressionInstanceCallLowerer`) — the same treatment the `println` path already gave containers, reusing the typed branches the native emitters already have. Proof RED-first: the new `NativeStringValueOfContainerE2ETest` (6 faces: `List<Int>`, `Map<String,Int>`, `Set<String>`, `List<String>`, a NESTED list, `end`) **fails 3 of its 4 target faces pre-fix** (x86-64/riscv64/aarch64 printed the garbage bytes) and passes all 4 after, byte-identical to the JVM oracle; the focused collection/print battery **311 run / 0F / 0E (4 skipped)**; the golden suite **284/284** (71 cases × 4 targets) (jar rebuilt with `-pl kof-cli -am package`). The queue drops 2 → 1 (§652, mitigated by the ASM 9.10.1 pin).
last: **known-bugs §678 CLOSED — the failing reference cast finally has ONE meaning on all four targets (13/10, lane compiler/JVM/native, owner `192.168.15.30:9092`)** — the two NATIVE emitters lowered `KofCheckCast` to a NO-OP, so `a as Dog` on a failing cast printed the softened value and exited 0, while the JVM (CHECKCAST, the oracle), the Script and the JS threw; that was the LAST open face of §678 (face A = `as T?` always-null landed 13/10 by this lane; the JVM/Script/JS faces by lane flatt 12-13/10). Fix: `NativeCastGuard` emits a `kof_cast_check(obj, typeId)` call for the targets that HAVE a runtime type id — strict for USER classes and `String` (the same scope as the interpreter's non-null face, so Script and native agree by construction), `null` PASSES (CHECKCAST semantics) and generics/`kof`-package containers pass through; the `java.lang` boxes are not a silent gap (they have no runtime type id, and the existing UNBOX check already aborts — measured: `Object = "hello"` then `as Int` exits non-zero on the JVM, on the Script AND on native). New runtime helper on both sides (`kof_cast_check` in `RuntimeMisc` for x86-64; `NativeRiscvAsmCast` as its OWN riscv slice, so the already-over-600 `NativeRiscvAsmRt0` is not grown), and the type-id resolution is now shared. Proof RED-first: `NativeCastFailE2ETest` measured **all four native faces RED** (x86-64 user-class, x86-64 String, riscv64, aarch64) and each one ABORTS like the JVM after the fix; the successful/null faces are byte-identical to the JVM oracle; the native/cast/generic/sealed/interface/ffi battery **1098 run / 0F / 0E (63 skipped)**; golden **284/284**. The open queue drops 3 → 2 (§652 mitigated, §686).
last: **known-bugs §687 FIXED — the riscv64 cross emitter no longer emits out-of-range `ld`/`sd` immediates on frames over ~2 KiB (13/10, lane compiler/JVM/native, owner `192.168.15.30:9092`)** — local slot `idx` lives at `-(idx+1)*8-16(s11)` and riscv load/store carry a 12-bit SIGNED immediate, so past ~254 locals the §625 slot-zeroing emitted `sd zero,-2056(s11)` and the ASSEMBLER rejected the build: the AVIF CFL probe (a 64x64 `Int[]` plus per-size arrays) could not be built for riscv64 AT ALL. Fix: `NativeRiscvCrossFrame.emitLocalMem` materializes the slot address in `t6` past the limit (`li t6, |off|` + `sub t6, s11, t6` + access at offset 0) and keeps the direct form below it, so small frames emit byte-identical asm; the frame responsibility moved out of `NativeRiscvCrossEmit`, which the guard had pushed past the 600-line CI limit. aarch64 needed NO change — it is generated by translating this same riscv text and that translator already materializes offsets past ±255 (measured: it consumes the new sequence). Proof RED-first: the new toolchain-free `RiscvLargeFrameAsmTest` fails **3 of its 4 faces** against the pre-fix emitter (`-2056`, the ledger's exact number) → 4/0F; §625's `PrologueSlotInitTest` 2/0F; the recorded repro `Av1CflE2ETest` **6 run / 0F / 0 skipped** — its riscv64 face, whose skip NAMED §687, RUNS again under qemu. The open queue drops 4 → 3 (§652, §678, §686).

last: **AVIF slice 3ai LANDED — `known-bugs` §682 FIXED, code REVERTED: the chroma planes are not yet composed into the tile driver; the blocker was the CHROMA ORACLE's decoder pass starting from the encoder's leftover entropy contexts — found + fixed 12/10 (encoder/decoder now agree symbol for symbol), the Kof chroma path was never implicated (12/10, lane pipeline/image-vision, owner `192.168.15.21:0`, flatt CLI)** — I had recorded that `av1TxbCtx`'s chroma `txb_skip_ctx` used a different formula than libaom's `get_txb_ctx_general` (`get_entropy_context(tx_size, a, l) + ctx_offset`), and MEASURED that FALSE: the two are algebraically identical (`ctx_offset` is 10 exactly when the plane block is bigger; `get_entropy_context` folds to `(above != 0) + (left != 0)`; so libaom's value is the same `7 + above + left + 3*bigger`). The `av1DecodeChromaBlock` composition (`libs/image/Av1DecodeChroma.kf`), the driver's chroma call, the chroma golden and the probe were REVERTED cleanly (`git status` empty; `Av1DecodeTileReconE2ETest` 7/7, `Av1DecodeTileTxTypeE2ETest` 7/7, `Av1UvTxSizeE2ETest` 8/8 green). last: **AVIF slice 3ag-c LANDED — `known-bugs` §680 FIXED: the AV1 intra tile driver reads the transform type BETWEEN `all_zero` and the coefficient levels (12/10, lane pipeline/image-vision, owner `192.168.15.21:0`, flatt CLI)** — the slice-3ag-b fix (§679) wired `readTxType` BEFORE `av1DecodeCoeffsBlockEdge`, but libaom's `av1_read_coeffs_txb` reads the `all_zero` flag FIRST and calls `av1_read_tx_type` only when it is 0, so on any transform block with a residual the driver consumed the wrong symbol and the entropy stream desynchronised from there (§680 had recorded that as a "wrong transform size for the 2nd and later blocks"; the tx-size-context hypothesis in that entry was DISPROVEN — Kof ctx == libaom ctx on every block). `BLOCK_32X32` never showed it (`TX_32X32` signals no `tx_type`) and the §679 golden's one-byte tile had all-zero symbols. Fix: `Av1Coeffs.decodeBlock` split into `readTxbSkip` + `setTxType` + `decodeLevels`; `Av1CoeffPlane.kf` gained `av1DecodeCoeffsBlockEdgeTx` reading `all_zero` → luma `tx_type` → levels; the driver calls it. New proof: `Av1DecodeTileTxTypeE2ETest` **7 run / 0F / 0 skipped** (JVM + Script + native x86-64 + riscv64/aarch64 qemu + JS) over a REGENERATED golden (`SHA256 5d40c024…`) whose case 1 is the four-block `BLOCK_16X16` plane that exposed the bug; non-regression `Av1DecodeTileReconE2ETest` 7/7, `Av1DecodeTileE2ETest` 7/7, `Av1DecodeCoeffPlaneE2ETest` 8/8, `Av1DecodeBlockE2ETest` 7/7, `Av1TxTypeE2ETest` 6/6 (42 run / 0F / 2 skipped).

last: **AVIF slice 3ag-b LANDED — `known-bugs` §679 FIXED: the AV1 intra tile driver now reads `tx_type` for intra transform blocks <= 16x16 (12/10, lane pipeline/image-vision, owner `192.168.15.21:0`, flatt CLI)** — `av1DecodeTileRecon` (`libs/image/Av1DecodeTileRecon.kf`) took a hardcoded `txType = 0`, so it never consumed the symbol libaom signals for every non-skipped intra transform block of size <= 16x16 (`av1_read_tx_type` at `av1/decoder/decodemv.c:626` from `av1_read_coeffs_txb` at `av1/decoder/decodetxb.c:158`; `TX_16X16` -> `EXT_TX_SET_DTT4_IDTX` -> 5 symbols; `TX_32X32` -> `DCTONLY` -> none, which is why the slice-3ag golden missed it). The driver now takes a `reducedTxSet` flag, an `Av1TxType` store, the frame `txTypes` map and a `txTypeOut` record, and per transform block calls `readTxType(...)` BEFORE `av1DecodeCoeffsBlockEdge`, threading the DECODED type into the coefficient walk AND `av1ReconBlockInto`. Oracle = the REAL libaom path (varying tx types), zero symbol mismatches, pinned `SHA256 42b7c972…`. New `Av1DecodeTileTxTypeE2ETest` **7 run / 0F / 0 skipped** (JVM + Script + native x86-64 + riscv64/aarch64 qemu + JS); non-regression `Av1DecodeTileReconE2ETest` 7/7. last: **AVIF slice 3ag-a AUDIT (docs-only, no code change) — `known-bugs` §679 OPEN: the slice-3ag AV1 intra tile driver never reads `tx_type` for intra transform blocks <= 16x16 (LANDED 12/10, lane pipeline/image-vision, owner `192.168.15.21:0`, flatt CLI)** — the 3ag golden only exercises `BLOCK_32X32` -> `TX_32X32` -> `get_ext_tx_types` = `EXT_TX_SET_DCTONLY` = 1 (no `tx_type` symbol), but libaom signals it for every non-skipped intra transform block of size <= 16x16 (`av1_read_tx_type` at `av1/decoder/decodemv.c:626` from `av1_read_coeffs_txb` at `av1/decoder/decodetxb.c:158`; `TX_16X16` -> `EXT_TX_SET_DTT4_IDTX` -> 5 symbols), so `libs/image/Av1DecodeTileRecon.kf`'s hardcoded `txType = 0` desynchronises a real bitstream at the next symbol; `libs/image/Av1TxType.kf`'s `readTxType` (slice 3t) already implements the read and is never called from the driver. Measured with a re-provisioned libaom oracle (pinned `d53e6d0b1`) that drives libaom's REAL `read_intra_frame_mode_info` + `read_tx_size` + `av1_read_coeffs_txb` + `av1_get_tx_type`: for a `BLOCK_16X16` block the decoder prints `ext=5` and reads 5 `intra_ext_tx_cdf` symbols while the encoder's stream has no `tx_type` write. last: AVIF slice 3ag (AV1 intra tile driver — mode-info + tx-size + coefficients interleaved per block — `libs/image/Av1DecodeTileRecon.kf`, new) LANDED 11/10 (lane pipeline/image-vision) — the stage libaom runs as `parse_decode_block`: per block, the mode info (3p/3q/3r) and the transform size (3s) are read, and THEN the same block's coefficients (3e/3x), availability (3ab), prediction (3u) and reconstruction (3z) run — before the next block, over ONE shared entropy stream. `av1DecodeTileRecon(...)` returns the reconstructed luma plane and records every transform block's coefficients and every block's mode info. Oracle = a C encoder writing the mode info, the transform size and the coefficients in the exact order libaom reads them with libaom's OWN default CDFs and range encoder, decoded by libaom's REAL `read_intra_frame_mode_info` + `read_tx_size` + `av1_read_coeffs_txb` + the `av1_predict_intra_block` builders + `av1_highbd_inv_txfm_add_c` over 3 cases (pinned `SHA256 202acbb3…`). `Av1DecodeTileReconE2ETest` 7 run / 0F / 2 skipped (riscv64/aarch64 RUN the same golden, `BLOCKED_BY_602` now false — §602 FIXED upstream 11/10). Non-regression: `Av1DecodeTileE2ETest` 7/7, `Av1DecodeTilePlaneE2ETest` 7/7, `Av1DecodeCoeffPlaneE2ETest` 7/7, `Av1DecodeReconBlockE2ETest` 7/7, `Av1ModeInfoE2ETest` 6/6, `Av1ModeBodyE2ETest` 6/6, `Av1ModeTailE2ETest` 6/6, `Av1PaletteE2ETest` 6/6, `Av1TxSizeE2ETest` 6/6, `Av1CoeffPlaneE2ETest` 7/7, `Av1CoeffPlaneEdgeE2ETest` 7/7, `Av1DecodeBlockE2ETest` 7/7. `decodeRaster` AVIF still refused until the chain closes.
last: **qrcode-wasm Phase 1 slice 2a LANDED — the QR file reader in pure Kof (12/10, lane nativa/GC, owner `192.168.15.117:0`)** — `libs/qrcode/QrDecode.kf` `decodeQr(path)` decodes a QR symbol from a PNG: pngRaster → dark bounding box → candidate scales validated by finder patterns + format BCH (bit order verified against a python-generated QR) → unmask → placement-order extraction → deinterleave with the writer's RS tables → per-block syndrome verification → byte-mode/UTF-8 parse. Proof (executed, red-first): round-trip 20/20 on JVM; `QrCodeReaderE2ETest` 7/7 (round-trip on Script/native x86-64/cross riscv64-aarch64, python-qrcode decode at scales 1-3, corruption and QR-free refusals); `QrCodeWriterE2ETest` 8/8 non-regression. Five real bugs found and fixed during the port: LSB-first format bits, 9x9 vs 8x8 reserved overcount, v2-6 remainder overflow, codeword-bytes-read-as-bits, message stream through per-block sequences (block-transition corruption at byte 29 of 100). Slice scope: upright digital images; rotations/other ECC levels/versions>10/damaged symbols refused by name.

last: **known-bugs §671 FIXED — same-arity constructor collision on the native backend (LANDED 12/10, lane nativa/GC, owner `192.168.15.117:0`)** — the native mangler keyed constructors by arity only (`Class_init_<arity>`), so two same-arity constructors with different parameter types emitted the same `.globl` and the assembler failed `symbol ... is already defined` (found by the image-vision lane while composing AVIF slice 3af). Fix: the constructor symbol gains the standard `sigTag` descriptor only on a same-arity conflict (single-constructor case unchanged, zero churn), applied together on the definition side and both call sites (x86 + riscv64/aarch64), with the call site using the resolved overload's types. Proof (executed, RED-first): `NativeConstructorOverloadE2ETest` 2F pre-fix (exact ledger error on x86 and cross riscv64) → 4/4 post-fix; non-regression 67/67 (ConstructorOverloadE2ETest 2, ConstructorArgTypeTest 6, MemorySafetyE2ETest 48, KofGcE2ETest 8, NativeCrossWideArgsE2ETest 3, PrologueSlotInitTest 2). Open queue: §652, §657, §659, §667.

A `String`-RETURN callback and both `String` faces on the cross riscv64/aarch64 stay honest `FFI001`. Docs EN+PT updated; gates rc=0; `mvn -o -pl kof-compiler -am compile` rc=0.

last: **`qrcode-wasm-plan` promoted out of `future/` (D-FUTURE-PROMOTION resumed per D-FUTURE-UNFREEZE) and Phase 1 slice 1 LANDED — the QR writer in pure Kof (11/10, lane nativa/GC, owner `192.168.15.117:0`)** — `libs/qrcode/Qrcode.kf` (ISO/IEC 18004 byte mode over UTF-8, versions 1-10, ECC M, auto version + spec N1-N4 mask penalty) + `libs/qrcode/QrPng.kf` (grayscale PNG, 4-module quiet zone, stored-deflate zlib, CRC-32 + Adler-32). Proof (executed): matrices byte-identical to the Python `qrcode` module (independent implementation) across an 80-case battery (20 + 60 randomized, 0 fails); PNGs decode back under zbar via ctypes; `QrCodeWriterE2ETest` 8/8 (JVM parity + zbar round-trip + Script + native x86-64 + JS + cross riscv64/aarch64 + oversized refusal); `FileLibraryE2ETest` 13/13. Three bugs caught against the reference during the port: RS interleave short by one byte on the two-group versions, data bits read LSB-first, N4 pre-floored percentage. Phase 1 continues with the file reader; Phase 3 (`KofWasm`) stays rule-6.
last: **memory-safety M1 doc-truth, second pass — the "only a `String`-return callback stays `FFI001`" claim corrected across 11 docs EN+PT (LANDED 11/10, lane docs/lab, owner `192.168.15.130:9093`)** — MEASURED that a `String` **argument** inside a callback is ALSO refused on Native: probe (`driver.compile`, temporary test removed) `extern "c" f(s: String, cb: (String) -> Int): Int` → `FFI001` on `Target.NATIVE` but `success=true` on `Target.JVM`, while an `(Int) -> Int` callback in the same shape binds on Native (the `String` scalar extern arg is fine — the refusal is the `String` INSIDE the callback). Cause: `FfiSignature.nativeCallbackDesc`/`crossCallbackDesc` accept only `{i,j,b,f,d,v}`, so a `String` arg (char `S`, bindable on JVM/JS per 3.4-C3.4) fails the same gate. Corrected EN+PT: `docs/spec/memory-safety.md` (§7 + §12), `docs/memory-safety-plan.md` (header + measured bullet), `docs/backend-parity.md`, `docs/ffi-abi-structs.md`, `docs/language-reference/{modules,syntax,specification-status}.md`, `docs/development/{roadmap,README}.md`, `docs/architecture/IMPLEMENTATION-UNIVERSAL-PLATFORM.md`, `docs/status.md`. Docs-only; gates rc=0; `mvn -o -pl kof-compiler -am compile` rc=0.
last: **`known-bugs` §602 CLOSED — the sixth face root-caused and fixed at the allocator; ALL faces green (LANDED 11/10, lane nativa/GC, owner `192.168.15.117:0`)** — the "missed mark" hypothesis was WRONG: zero-injection RSP debugging on the repo riscv64 ELF (a minimal GDB remote-serial client; gdb-multiarch not installable here) + a lockstep trace (GC-on vs GC-disabled ELF, synchronized allocation-by-allocation) proved the mark EXACT; the divergence is in the VALUES of reconstruction writes (same `kof_array_set` call site wrote `frame[175]=149` GC-on vs `127` no-GC). Root: the riscv64 free-list reuse path (`kof_alloc` `.Lkof_alloc_found`) never received the x86 §541 payload-zeroing — a reused block keeps the previous life's bytes while `new Int[n]` must be zero-initialized (JVM oracle), and `av1ReconBlockLevels` writes only 1 of 64 elements (eob semantics). Fix: `NativeRiscvAsmRtB42` zeroes the reused payload (8-byte word loop over size−32); aarch64 inherits. Proof (executed): sweep 12/12 riscv64 + 12/12 aarch64 (was 9 corrupted/arch); `Av1DecodeReconBlockE2ETest` 7/7 with the assumption machinery removed; `Av1FilterIntraE2ETest` 6/0F (149.8s), `Av1IntraBlockE2ETest` 6/0F (162.3s), `Av1CoeffCtxE2ETest` 6/0F (63.6s); the SEVENTH face (slice 3ad) `BLOCKED_BY_602` gate flipped → `Av1DecodeCoeffPlaneE2ETest` 8/8, 0 skipped; battery KofGcE2ETest 8/8 + MemorySafetyE2ETest 48/48 + MemoryClearE2ETest 2/2 + PrologueSlotInitTest 2/2. Live-bug queue is now §652 (JVM-backend lane) + §657 (shared-builtin call path).

A real compiler bug caught (`known-bugs` §671): two SAME-ARITY constructors collide on native (`Class_init_<arity>`); the driver stays native-clean with a single `Av1Symbol` constructor. `Av1DecodeTileE2ETest` 7 run / 0F / 2 skipped (riscv64/aarch64 SKIP, `BLOCKED_BY_602`). last: AVIF slice 3ae (AV1 heterogeneous intra plane driver — `libs/image/Av1DecodeTilePlane.kf`, new) LANDED 11/10 (lane pipeline/image-vision) — the composition libaom runs for the blocks of a real tile: a plane of SEVERAL mode-info blocks, each with its OWN block size, transform size, transform type and intra mode, decoded in tile order over ONE shared entropy stream (one reader, one adapting coefficient-CDF store, one above/left entropy-context pair). `av1DecodeTilePlaneRecon(frame, stride, frameW, frameH, blocks, bitDepth, disableEdgeFilter, intraEdgeFilterType, dcQuant, acQuant, reader, store, above, left, levelsOut)` walks `blocks` (`(r, c, bs, tx, tt, mode)` per block): per block it enumerates the block's transform blocks (slice 3y), decodes the coefficients (slices 3e/3x) with THIS block's tx size/type, derives availability from the block's ABSOLUTE position (slice 3ab), predicts with THIS block's mode (slice 3u) and reconstructs into the plane (slice 3z). Oracle = the REAL libaom `av1_read_coeffs_txb` (`allow_update_cdf = 1`) + `has_top_right`/`has_bottom_left` from the block's ABSOLUTE `mi_row`/`mi_col` + `highbd_build_*_intra_predictors` + `av1_highbd_inv_txfm_add_c` over 7 heterogeneous cases (pinned `SHA256 5d78218b…`). **A real defect caught and fixed (RED-proven):** `av1IntraAvail` (slice 3ab) derived the `xr`/`yd` edge distances from `frameW - wpx` WITHOUT subtracting the block's own `miCol`/`miRow`, so it under-counted `nTopPx`/`nLeftPx` for every block not at the plane origin (libaom's `set_mi_row_col` sets `mb_to_right_edge = (mi_cols - bw - mi_col) * MI_SIZE * 8`); the fix subtracts the position and the 3ab oracle's coverage prologue now drives the off-origin `mi_row`/`mi_col` grid, which re-derives 379 of the 5000 rows differently — the regenerated golden (`SHA256 af05d4e6…`) makes `Av1IntraAvailE2ETest` RED (6/7 FAIL) on the old code. `Av1DecodeTilePlaneE2ETest` 7 run / 0F / 2 skipped: JVM+Script+x86-64+JS exact; the riscv64/aarch64 faces SKIP with the reason named (`BLOCKED_BY_602`, the `known-bugs` §602 intra-plane reclaim). Non-regression `Av1IntraAvailE2ETest` 7/7 (regenerated golden), `Av1DecodeCoeffPlaneE2ETest` 7/7, `Av1DecodeReconBlockE2ETest` 7/7, `Av1DecodeBlockE2ETest` 7/7, `Av1AvailE2ETest` 7/7. `decodeRaster` AVIF still refused until the chain closes.

last: **`known-bugs` §602 — the AV1 cross hang faces root-caused and fixed; §602 remains open on the sixth face (LANDED 11/10, lane nativa/GC, owner `192.168.15.117:9092`)** — the compound fix's `size >= 64` sanity in `kof_gc_try_mark` (`NativeRiscvAsmRtB43`; aarch64 inherits line-by-line) rejected every real block smaller than 64 bytes, and the allocator's minimum real block is **48 bytes** (the ≤16-byte request class = `kof_list_new`'s 16-byte backing array, the payload of every `listOf`): live 48-byte blocks were swept while alive, the next `kof_alloc` re-used the memory, and with list data corrupted the data-dependent AV1 loops never terminated. Fix: floor 64→48; ghost-block defenses intact, x86 parity restored. Proof (executed): FI probe 1090 lines/70 calls byte-equal to the JVM golden; `Av1FilterIntraE2ETest` 6/0F, `Av1IntraBlockE2ETest` 6/0F, `Av1CoeffCtxE2ETest` 6/0F, `Av1CoeffCdfE2ETest` 6/0F; non-regression battery 88/0F; `mvn -o -pl kof-compiler -am compile` rc=0. The sixth face (`Av1DecodeReconBlockE2ETest` cross) re-measured with the `BLOCKED_BY_602` assumption lifted: still 2F — the 48-floor does not reach it, assumption re-set. The live-bug queue is §602 (sixth face) + §652 (JVM-backend lane; ASM 9.11 `ComputeLimits`) + §655 (2-arg `String.split` — FIXED 11/10, lane flatt `192.168.100.14:0`).

last: AVIF slice 3x-edge (plane-edge clamp of the AV1 coefficient plane decode driver — `libs/image/Av1CoeffPlane.kf`) LANDED 10/10 (lane pipeline/image-vision) — closes the gap the slice-3x entry recorded: libaom `av1_set_entropy_contexts` (`av1/common/blockd.c`) clamps the above/left context store to the plane's right/bottom edge (`mb_to_right_edge`/`mb_to_bottom_edge` < 0 → `max_block_wide`/`max_block_high`), so a block whose transform footprint overhangs the plane stores the packed context only over the units INSIDE the plane and zeroes the rest; the next block's `get_txb_ctx` then reads a zero neighbour past the edge, not the last block's context. All five slice-3x golden grids were exactly plane-sized, so the branch was never exercised. New `av1DecodeCoeffsPlaneEdge(reader, store, planeBsize, plane, grid, above, left, planeBlocksWide, planeBlocksHigh)` + `av1DecodeCoeffsBlockEdge(..., planeBlocksWide, planeBlocksHigh)` carry the clamp (`0` = no edge); `av1DecodeCoeffsPlane`/`av1DecodeCoeffsBlock` delegate with `0, 0` (behaviour identical). Oracle = the REAL libaom formula: the real `block_size_wide`/`tx_size_wide_unit` tables and the real `get_txb_ctx` (compiled from source on the dev host) over four overhanging grids (`eR` right-only, `eB` bottom-only, `eC` corner, `e8` an 8x8 grid overhanging by one unit), zero mismatches, pinned gzip+base64 (`SHA256 d0bcf587…`). Cross native faces run one plane per process (`known-bugs` §602). No compiler change, no new gap, no decision. `decodeRaster` still refuses AVIF until the chain closes.
last: AVIF slice 3x (AV1 coefficient plane decode driver — `libs/image/Av1CoeffPlane.kf`, new) LANDED 09/10 (lane pipeline/image-vision) — the glue that ties the slice-3w neighbour-context selection to the slice-3e coefficient walk across the transform-block grid of a plane, matching libaom `av1_read_coeffs_txb` + `av1_set_entropy_contexts` (`av1/decoder/decodetxb.c`, `av1/common/blockd.c`). `av1DecodeCoeffsPlane( reader, store, planeBsize, plane, grid, above, left )` walks a flattened `(r4, c4, txSz, txType)` grid (length 4×N): per block it slices the plane's `above[c4..c4+twu-1]`/`left[r4..r4+thu-1]` contexts, calls `av1TxbCtx` for `skipCtx`/`dcSignCtx`, runs `Av1Coeffs.decodeBlock`, packs the result with `av1TxbEntropyContext` and stores it back over the block's `txbWUnit`/`txbHUnit` 4x4 units — the state the next block's context reads — returning the per-block signed coefficient arrays.
